Avoiding Chemo in Breast Cancer; Psychedelic Medication for Depression

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

— Also in TTHealthWatch: treatment of Achilles tendinitis

TTHealthWatch is a weekly podcast from Texas Tech. In it, Elizabeth Tracey, director of electronic media for Johns Hopkins Medicine in Baltimore, and Rick Lange, MD, president of Texas Tech Health El Paso, look at the top medical stories of the week.

This week's topics include treatment of Achilles tendinitis, avoiding chemo in some types of breast cancer, imaging and cardiac stent placement, and a psychedelic medication in depression.

2:38 Treatment-resistant depression and psychedelic medicine

3:38 Three dosages and psychological support

4:38 Adverse events same among all groups

7:00 One in 13 people have Achilles tendinopathy

8:00 Initial improvement with steroids then rapid decline

9:10 HER2-positive early breast cancer giving chemo a miss

11:10 A third responded just to antibodies

Elizabeth: Can we avoid chemotherapy in some women with HER2-positive breast cancer?

Rick: Are injections helpful for people with Achilles tendon problems?

Elizabeth: Can we use a psychedelic medication to help treatment-resistant depression?

Rick: And using imaging to guide stent placement in people with coronary artery disease.

Elizabeth: That's what we're talking about this week on TTHealthWatch, your weekly look at the medical headlines from Texas Tech University Health Sciences Center in El Paso. I'm Elizabeth Tracey, a Baltimore-based medical journalist.

Rick: And I'm Rick Lange, president of Texas Tech Health El Paso.

Elizabeth: Rick, how about if we turn right to the New England Journal of Medicine? This study appears to me to be having some rather large implications for clinical practice.

Rick: This is for people that are going to have a percutaneous coronary intervention because they have coronary artery disease. If you do additional imaging during the procedure, you actually get a better result. That imaging is usually intravascular ultrasound. Use of intravascular imaging is associated with a lower risk of adverse clinical events that require an additional procedure. You don't really have all the data until you're actually in the middle of the procedure.

Well, a lot of people now are getting the coronary CT. It can provide a non-invasive assessment of the coronary artery disease. Since that's done before the procedure, can we use that information to guide therapy? Will it be as useful as using intravascular ultrasound?

So this was an international randomized non-inferiority trial; 1,100 patients that had had coronary CT and they knew they needed to have a procedure done. Half of them were randomized to just using that information to guide stenting. And the other half, in addition, had intravascular ultrasound. And they assessed at 12 months what the rate of target vessel failure was. Those in the CTA [CT angiography] group, about 4.1% had target vessel failure. In the intravascular ultrasound, about 4.6%. The CT was non-inferior to the intravascular ultrasound.

Elizabeth: And a lot easier to do, sounds like, in providing this information a priori versus having it concomitant with the procedure.

Rick: It is, Elizabeth. It requires several things. You have the expertise and the equipment to do this and do it well. Number two is, remember, it exposes the people to radiation. Now, what the study didn't do was it didn't do a cost analysis.

Elizabeth: That, of course, was going to be exactly my next question. And it just seems to me, and I could be wrong on this, but, gosh, CT is just so routine and so quick that I suspect it's less expensive. But I don't know that.

Rick: Yeah. And the other thing I should mention, Elizabeth, this was done at elective procedures.

Elizabeth: OK. Let's turn from here to JAMA, this issue of treatment-resistant depression, a single intranasal administration of the psychedelic mebufotenin for treatment-resistant depression.

This medicine, I should note, is actually a synthetic modification of this toad-derived venom that we've known for a long time has actually had these properties. In this case, they looked at this in participants who were 18 to 75 years of age with moderate to severe major depressive disorder that had been nonresponsive to previous attempts, two to five of them, to treat it with current antidepressant medicines. They were looking at two different dosages of this medicine, as I mentioned, administered intranasally, 8 mg and 12 mg. And their comparator was a 0.3 mg administration alongside with psychological support.

Their primary endpoint was a total score change in the Montgomery-Åsberg Depression Rating Scale from baseline to day 29. And then they had a number of secondary endpoints. They also note that their discharge readiness in comparison with other psychedelic medications, like psilocybin for example, was assessed starting 90 minutes after their dosing.

Basically, what they found, it was a little bit curious. This medicine did seem to have an impact. They called it durable. Their day 29 response rates were 35%, 34%, and 12% with regard to 8 mg, 12 mg, and the comparator 0.3 mg dose. Their remission rates were 26%, 18%, and 8% in that same dosing regimen. It's a little curious, isn't it? Because the 12 [mg] appears to have not quite as good results as the 8 mg dose. They also did note that there were adverse events, but these were more or less the same among all of the doses. The editorialist opines that in comparison to what we already know about things like ketamine and psilocybin, maybe this is something that might add to the armamentarium.

Rick: These individuals, to test the effect of the drug, had to be off their antidepressants. These are people that already have refractory depression. They take them off their antidepressants and they only treat them with this, and then they follow them over the course of 60 days. The fact that a third had some response and a fourth had complete remission, for somebody that has refractory depression and on no other antidepressants, they may actually not be bad results.

Once you treat people with a psychedelic, whether it be this or psilocybin or LSD, you have to observe them until the psychedelic effects wear off. With the other drugs, it's 4 to 12 hours to have to be sitting around waiting. With this particular medication, most people go home in 2 hours. With regard to durability, it lasted for at least 60 days, a single dose. Pretty darn good.

Will it last longer than 60 days? What is the best dose strategy? What if you combine it with other antidepressants? And the last thing I would want to say, Elizabeth, is the people that were excluded from this study were people that have suicidal ideation because there's concern that the psychedelic drugs in individuals with depression and suicidal ideation may actually make those worse.

Elizabeth: The editorialist, of course, also points out the suicidality as an issue, as an exclusion criterion in this trial. So those people clearly need to be examined and studied carefully. The editorialist notes that the average patient became only about a third better. Remission rates were below those typically seen with ketamine and ECT [electroconvulsive therapy]. So since those things exist already, maybe it's important to look really carefully at the practicality of another treatment strategy here.

Rick: Without direct head-to-head comparisons, patients could be different. We would actually need to compare them head to head. The other therapies you mentioned require some expertise and some equipment sometimes. If you could pop someone into your office, give them an intranasal application of a drug that could be effective, even if only in a quarter of individuals, and people would be gone in 2 hours, that's at least another tool in our armamentarium of treating major depression.

Rick: I was unaware that as many as one in 13 individuals have Achilles tendinopathy sometimes. Although exercise treatment is oftentimes recommended, about half the individuals do not get better and almost half these people end up getting additional treatments such as injections. They're putting 50 mL of saline between the Achilles tendon and what's called Kager's fat pad.

These injections sometimes have just saline, sometimes they have steroids, and sometimes they have a local anesthetic. And the thought is that there's a mechanical or ischemic disruption of the pain signaling and the steroid has anti-inflammatory effects. Is it really effective? Nobody has ever tested that before.

So 192 adults with Achilles tendinopathy received a sham small-dose injection, the high-volume injection with steroids, or a high-volume injection without steroids. And then they followed them about a year. There were no clear differences between the sham and the high-volume injections, either with or without steroids. It looked like there was an initial improvement in the people that got injections with steroids, but then there was a rapid decline.

However, when they looked at cost-effectiveness, it looked like the high-dose injection with corticosteroids was cost-effective. I kind of scratched my head and said, gosh, if there's no difference, how could that be more cost-effective? Well, during that initial 12 weeks where it looked like it was a little bit better, that's when people were able to return to work earlier. Even though at the end there was no difference, the fact that they were able to return to work a little bit earlier made it a little bit more cost-effective.

Elizabeth: Sounding like it's back to the drawing board for folks with this condition. I'm wondering, does it talk about the natural history of it? How long does it take to resolve in most people in the absence of treatment?

Rick: Really interesting, because they follow these individuals over the course of about a year. Most of the improvement occurred, even in those that had sham, somewhere between 3 and 6 months. It's a long time. The primary treatment is exercise and anti-inflammatory medications. Those take 3 to 6 months to work, so people have to be patient.

Elizabeth: Let's turn to Nature Medicine and let's look at this idea that, gosh, there are some women who might be able to forego chemotherapy in their HER2-positive early breast cancer. This is a trial that's been ongoing for a while and this is looking at the 5-year outcomes. The study initiated as a noncomparative phase II trial evaluating PET-guided pathological complete response [pCR]-adapted adaptation strategy to omit chemotherapy in women with HER2-positive early breast cancer. I say early, that was stages I through IIIA, and the breast cancer was operable. They were randomized to receive chemotherapy plus trastuzumab [Herceptin] and pertuzumab [Perjeta], or some of them actually ended up getting early chemotherapy, but just a couple rounds of it, and then endocrine therapy.

In the second group, their treatment was adapted according to their FDG-PET response after 2 cycles of chemotherapy and pCR. They previously reported that this de-escalation revealed a 3-year invasive disease-free survival of 94.3%. Here, they're looking at 5-year results using circulating tumor DNA findings.

After their median follow-up of 5.6 years, that number was almost 90%, 89.5% in that group who were randomized to have their circulating tumor DNA assessed along with the FDG-PET results. They only showed in that group one distant recurrence. What they're suggesting is that this might be a really important strategy for being able to look at women who might be able to forego that chemotherapy.

Rick: Standard therapy is you give chemotherapy with [a taxane and a platinum], and you also combine these HER2 antibodies, the two that you mentioned. OK. Maybe we can forego the chemotherapy if, in fact, people would just respond to the HER2 antibodies. That's what they showed. And about a third of those patients did respond just to the antibodies and it saved them the toxic side effects of chemotherapy. This is pretty amazing. Anytime we can minimize the toxicity, get a terrific result, that's great news.

Elizabeth: They do note that this notion of circulating tumor DNA at baseline or during treatment, when they were able to find out, was associated with inferior disease-free survival. What this suggests to me is that looking for the tumor's DNA in the blood has to really be employed as a clinical marker for both "this might be a strategy that's going to work for you" as well as "what's going on on an ongoing basis."

Rick: Yeah. And so, Elizabeth, you're right. They use two different techniques. One is imaging. The other used serum biomarkers. If your imaging looked like the tumor had resolved and there were no biomarkers, those were the best outcomes.

Elizabeth: I'm going to go out on a limb here and prognosticate that at some point, I think, that circulating tumor DNA will supplant imaging because, as we're well aware, frequently imaging misses a lot of metastasis in particular that can be in certain spots in the body where it's difficult to discern.

Rick: And I would suggest that both will be helpful.

Elizabeth: I think both for a while, and I'm guessing it will supplant. We'll see what happens.

On that note then, that's a look at this week's medical headlines from Texas Tech. I'm Elizabeth Tracey.

The Velotric GoMad Electric Utility eBike — CleanTechnica Tested

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Velotric has emerged as one of the largest ebike brands in the US, offering a wide range of models for buyers to choose from. Having a wide range of models with differing capabilities is increasingly a key factor for buyers when deciding which ebike to buy, as it allows them to upgrade or swap out for a different model to accommodate different stages of life.

They reached out to CleanTechnica about a review and we were excited to play with the Velotric GoMad Electric Utility bike, because it is just so darn flexible in what it can do. You can throw a set of bags on the back rack or a passenger kit to bring a friend with you. You can put a basket on the back and deliver pizzas, or you can just throw a backpack on and ride around town solo. It truly looked like the jack of all trades, so we pulled the trigger, and after a few days, it showed up.

Disclaimer: Velotric provided the GoMad to the author for the purposes of this review.

Assembling the Velotric GoMad is similar to the process for most e-bikes. It can be done with a basic set of tools that are included with the bike, and takes between 30 minutes and an hour depending on how much of a hurry you’re in.

After that, it’s a simple matter of attaching the pedals, mounting the handlebars to the stem, installing the front wheel and tire, connecting a few of the electrical connectors between the handlebars and the bike itself, airing up the tires, and you’re on your way. Ours took a few minutes longer than normal to bolt on the optional Passenger Kit

Taking in the Velotric GoMad, it is an attractive bike with great proportions. The battery is integrated into the main downtube on the frame, which makes it look slightly bloated, but in a way that fits into the overall proportions of the bike. The oversized tires help with this, making it look like it can go absolutely anywhere.

The rear hub motor kicks out plenty of power, and with a range of pedal assist modes. You can choose just how much power you need for the ride you want. You can choose from the motor being completely off, Eco, Tour, Trail, Sport, and even the full-out Boost mode that can give you exactly the support you need.

It’s perfect for when you’re carrying a load, powering up a hill, or just feeling tired. The motor is always ready to step in to provide assistance. At the upper end of its power cycle, the motor controller is more sensitive to the pedaling cadence, resulting in the motor pulsing on and off, providing for a bit of a bumpy sensation when pedaling. It’s only something you’d notice if you’re really paying attention, but it’s there.

The GoMad is designed with an integrated rear rack and a hidden storage pocket. The rack can also be converted into a seat for a second passenger with Velotric’s passenger kit. The passenger kit itself is amazing if that’s what you’re looking for, and this is further accentuated by the bike’s compact footprint. The tradeoff is that the main seat has a flat rear, which does make it slightly less comfortable than more fully built-out options.

The passenger kit comes with a set of pegs down below for the passengers’ feet to rest on, a rear grab rail for the rack, and a front handle for the passenger to hang onto as you’re boosting around town. Given the compact footprint of the bike, it feels like it was designed to be used as more of a teenager-plus-one setup, but if you want to put it to work as a husband-and-wife date night setup, its 500 pound weight capacity is more than up to the task.

I rode around on it with my wife on the back and it was very comfortable and stable for both of us. The foot pegs and the solid support bars that come with the passenger kit make it feel much safer and more comfortable for the passenger. I took a few pics of us on the bike, but, hilariously, none of them had the bike in frame and she wasn’t a fan of my one handed stunts. Of course, that’s for you to decide what you want to do with your life and how you want to set up your bike and put it to use.

The Velotric Nomad is an extremely capable bike at a mid-level price point for bikes of this quality and capability. It’s currently listed as $1,899 with a special Black Friday offer that includes a free young passenger kit.

The fact that its 750W continuous power output motor has a peak of 1,300W means it’s got plenty of pep to take even the largest and least-in-shape riders wherever they want to go. The specs across the board and the integration of the torque and cadence sensor make it feel like a very natural ride that will surprise you with just how intuitively the power is coming from the motor.

I especially love the colorways this bike comes in. They have a nice modern-looking sage that feels classy and refined, a dark amber that’s somewhere between a bright orange and bright red that looks like a pop of lipstick that really sets the whole frame off, and finally an obsidian, which is what our review bike came in.

The obsidian has a base color that looks almost black, but then when you put it in the sunlight, it has a rainbow pop of color that peeks out every once in a while. It’s not so much that it feels like it’s covered in glitter, but it’s just enough to make it feel fun.

For more information about the Velotric Nomad e-bike, head over to their official website, where you can learn all about it or purchase one for yourself.

Wendy’s Just Released a Video Game Where You Can Win Fast Food

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

I won a medium fries by defeating a sinister robot that looked suspiciously similar to a McDonald’s french fry container.

Wendy’s has turned its red-headed mascot into a wisecracking warrior who battles evil french fry robots in a free mobile game called WendyQuest. The company announced the retro-style role-playing game on Nov. 4. The announcement came with a promotion where you can win codes in the game, which are redeemable for menu items like fries and burgers at Wendy’s locations.

WendyQuest is available to download on iOS and Android. Desktop users can get it through the Steam platform. Anyone can play the game, but the promotion is restricted to people who are 18 and up living in the US.

You need to sign up for a Wendy’s account to redeem a code. While the food you can win is free, it requires you to buy something when you go.

“Wendy’s has been a part of gaming culture for years, including on Twitch, Fortnite and Feast of Legends, but WendyQuest is the brand’s first fully owned video game,” a spokesperson for Wendy’s said.

In 2019, Wendy’s partnered with the online multiplayer video game Fortnite, as well as the video-streaming platform Twitch, for various promotions featuring the company’s mascot. The company even released a rule book for a traditional tabletop roleplaying game the same year, called Feast of Legends, as a free download.

I downloaded WendyQuest, and my daughter and I played it for a while. The blocky graphics are reminiscent of the home-console video games of the late 1980s and early 1990s. It plays a lot like a simplified version of the DragonQuest, Final Fantasy and Breath of Fire games from the era, only stuffed with fast-food branding.

Rather than the regular turn-based battles that come standard in the early RPGs, this game adds a timing element, where you need to press a button at the right time like you do in old golfing video games.

It’s not a bad game. The light humor is suitable for younger players, and it’s fun to play. Like many RPGs, you battle monsters to gain levels, search around the maps for hidden areas, buy items and talk to people in towns to figure out what’s going on.

It took us over an hour to complete the first region, Fryshire, and get the Fry Stone, which unlocks a promo code for medium fries, free with a purchase. But again, you have to sign up for a Wendy’s account on their website or on the company’s mobile app.

Video games have a long history of offering real-world prizes to players as promotions. Swordquest, a series of video games released in the early 1980s for the Atari 2600, famously held a contest where players tried to win actual treasure made of gold and jewels.

In 2015, a promotion for the video game Just Cause 3 offered players the chance to win a private island. Developer Square Enix clarified that it couldn’t guarantee that the island would be livable and that the winner would have to pay all the taxes involved.

The reward codes from WendyQuest expire seven days after you get them, and Wendy’s says the codes are first come, first served.

Nico Paz, l'erede di Messi a San Siro: l'Inter ritrova il talento sfumato sul mercato

Domani l'Inter sfiderà il Como di Fabregas dopo la goleada in Champions contro il Lens. A guidare il Como ci sarà il talento Nico Paz, finito in estate nel mirino della squadra di Chivu.

"Ci sono sere che profumano di sentenza e stadi che non ammettono repliche. Quando domani Nico Paz calpesterà l’erba di San Siro per il big match tra Inter e Como, le tribune del Meazza si accenderanno per l’ennesimo esame di maturità, nell’aula magna del calcio italiano. Per il trequartista 22enne non si tratta di un debutto emozionale, ma del ritorno alla Scala del calcio da leader assoluto, nel pieno di una svolta epocale per la sua carriera: l’Argentina, che raggiungerà nella pausa, lo ha infatti formalmente indicato come l’erede designato per raccogliere la maglia numero 10, rimasta orfana dopo il ritiro dalla Seleccion di Messi, e il ruolo del grande Leo", sottolinea La Gazzetta dello Sport.

"Sul prato di San Siro l’Inter si troverà di fronte un calciatore che sta attraversando il momento migliore della giovane carriera. Il rendimento espresso finora sotto la guida tecnica di Fabregas parla chiaro: 6 gol e 8 assist in 13 presenze tra Serie A e Champions. L’ultimo acuto mercoledì notte nell’incredibile 9-1 al Lens, in cui Paz ha guidato la manovra offensiva firmando una rete e due passaggi vincenti. Un double che per Nico è diventato la norma. Nelle ultime cinque partite giocate, in ben 4 ha fatto gol e assist. Fabregas punta su di lui per sfatare il tabù: mai vittorioso in sei sfide contro l’Inter; mentre il Como, dopo il primo successo nel 1950 a San Siro, ha perso tutti i 14 incontri seguenti, e in ben otto di questi non è riuscito a segnare. A colpire gli addetti ai lavori è la straordinaria pulizia tecnica nella protezione del pallone del talento nato a Tenerife, e la visione di gioco palla al piede, qualità che lo rendono il fulcro creativo assoluto della formazione lariana e un pericolo costante per le linee difensive avversarie".

"La partita di domani rappresenta la perfetta chiusura del cerchio. San Siro, da sempre la Scala del calcio e teatro delle grandi notti di tanti assi argentini del presente e del passato, è il palcoscenico ideale per testare la crescita di un profilo simile. L’Inter cercherà punti fondamentali per evitare il sorpasso e difendere lo scudetto, ma l’attenzione del pubblico del Meazza sarà inevitabilmente catturata dal ragazzo con la faccia pulita e il sinistro fatato. Per il popolo nerazzurro ci sarà anche un pizzico di inevitabile malinconia. Durante la scorsa estate, infatti, il nome di Nico Paz era rimbalzato a lungo nei desideri della tifoseria, che sognava di vederlo inserito nella nuova mediana di Chivu per portare freschezza, estro e imprevedibilità ai campioni d’Italia. Quella suggestione è rimasta tale, e domani il trequartista si presenterà a Milano da avversario temutissimo. La parola adesso passerà unicamente al campo, dove Nico cercherà l’ennesima consacrazione italiana nello stadio più prestigioso, per dimostrare al mondo che l’Argentina ha davvero trovato il proprietario del suo nuovo, leggendario numero dieci."

Syncing directories between Linux servers from your phone: visual rsync with live command preview & dry-run

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Every homelab admin knows this moment: you want to sync a directory between two boxes, and you pause. Was it rsync -av src dst or rsync -av src/ dst? That one trailing slash decides whether you copy the folder into the target or mirror its contents — and combined with --delete, getting it wrong means rsync will happily remove files in your backup directory to "mirror" the wrong source.

I've typed these commands for years and I still pause before hitting Enter. A phone makes it worse: tiny keyboard, easy to fat-finger a path, no comfortable way to double-check before running. So in XuTerm, my Android SSH client, I built a visual rsync panel where the dangerous semantics are made explicit instead of memorized.

The panel is switches on top, but the exact rsync command is always visible at the bottom of the screen, updating live as you toggle options:

No invented shorthand, no hidden -e ssh, no "smart" rewriting. What you see is byte-for-byte what will execute. If you already know rsync, there's nothing to unlearn — the UI is just a flag composer that happens to be touch-friendly.

The trailing-slash toggle isn't just labeled --delete. Its description reads:

Sync directory contents (trailing /) — sync the contents of the source dir with --delete; all extra files in the target dir are removed.

One sentence, and you know exactly what will happen to files that exist on the target but not the source. The other options follow the same rule — each toggle maps to one flag, stated plainly:

--dry-run is a first-class toggle, and the recommended workflow is always: dry-run first → check the summary → run for real.

Syncing within one host is easy. Syncing across hosts means the app reaches two machines that may be reachable in different ways — one on LAN, one through a public IP. The panel handles this with:

Backups are the most repetitive task in a homelab. Every sync can be saved as a template (hosts + paths + switches), reloaded in one tap, and re-run a month later without re-reading flags. The monthly "is my backup still working?" check becomes: load template → dry-run → glance at the summary → run.

The rsync panel ships in XuTerm on Google Play — free, local-first, no account, your SSH keys never leave the device. If there's an rsync flag you'd want as a toggle, the comments are open.

Talk to Your Coding Agent: What Cursor's Realtime Voice Chat Actually Changes

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cursor's November 6 changelog entry is short — "Voice Chat with Agents" — but it changes the shape of a coding session. You can now talk with your agents in a realtime voice call: describe a change out loud, ask what an agent is working on, and hear updates as the work moves forward. Here is what the feature actually does, where it works, and where voice helps — and where it does not.

Voice chat works in the Agents Window, the IDE, on the web, and in Cursor for iOS. To start a call, wait until the agent is idle and the chat input is empty, then click the waveform button labeled Start voice chat. It sits where Send usually is, next to the microphone button for dictation.

On iOS, you can also say "Hey Siri, call Cursor" to start a call with a new agent, and mute or hang up from the Lock Screen with a Live Activity.

The important part is that the conversation does not stop when the agent starts working. While that agent runs, you can keep talking: ask what changed in a diff, check on a run, search earlier messages and calls with this agent, or switch between workers in a Project that has them. The voice agent listens, replies, and hands coding work to the agent in your chat.

The coding agent also speaks for itself. It shares short progress notes while it works and a brief answer when it is done, so you usually hear a short update instead of the full written reply. That design choice matters: voice is a summary channel, not a transcript channel.

Voice chat runs on Grok Voice. You can pick from many voices in Voice settings, including Ara, Eve, Leo, Rex, and Sal. With Privacy Mode on, voice chat runs with zero data retention — a detail worth noting if you work in a codebase with sensitive code or client data, since voice input is one more surface where data can leak.

Cursor is explicit that voice chat and dictation are two separate features. Dictation, which earlier releases called Voice Mode, still turns your speech into text in the chat input, and Cmd+Shift+Space still toggles it in the IDE. Dictation is speech-to-text; voice chat is a two-way conversation where the agent answers out loud.

That distinction decides which one to reach for. If you want to write a precise prompt — a function signature, an exact error message, a file path — dictation puts your words into the box and you can edit them before sending. If you want to stay in flow while an agent works — asking what it just changed while you look at something else — voice chat keeps the loop open without pulling your hands back to the keyboard.

1. Reviewing while the agent works. Asking "what changed in that diff?" out loud while the run is still going turns dead waiting time into review time. You are interrogating the work as it lands, instead of reading a finished summary after the fact.

2. Hands-busy moments. Walking through a design decision, standing at a whiteboard, or working on a second machine — anywhere typing a follow-up would break your train of thought, a spoken question keeps the agent moving.

3. Multi-worker projects. Switching between workers by voice, and searching earlier messages and calls with an agent, makes the conversation history a working memory you can query instead of a log you have to scroll.

Cursor shipped app building in the same week (its November 5 changelog lets you describe an app and get a deployed, shareable link), and voice chat is the other half of that direction: less time in the editor, more time directing. The IDE is becoming a place where you supervise agents rather than type every keystroke yourself — and the interface for supervision is starting to be a conversation, not a text box.

Whether that is faster depends on the task. For exploratory work, long refactors, and review-while-it-runs, talking to the agent is plausibly quicker than typing. For precise, surgical edits, the keyboard is not going anywhere. Update Cursor to try voice chat — and start with a task where a short spoken answer is enough.

Source: Cursor changelog, "Voice Chat with Agents" (November 6, 2026): https://cursor.com/changelog/voice-chat — re-check the original for the latest platform availability and voice options.

Extra Time and Exam Language: What AI Certification Policies Say About Accommodation Requests

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 7 • Originally published at academies.ai

Originally published on academies.ai: https://academies.ai/guides/ai-exam-accommodations-language/

Across the 4 vendor policy routes compared as shown in the United States on 15 October 2026, candidates must follow provider-specific portals or support channels rather than assuming a single standard rule. AWS, Microsoft, and Google Cloud require disability accommodation steps before scheduling, whereas NVIDIA requests an exam reservation code in a support case. AWS says ESL +30 needs to be requested once before registration and applies to future exam registrations with all test-delivery providers, as shown in the United States on 15 October 2026. Microsoft states that processing and scheduling an online or in-person accommodation request can take up to 20 days as shown in the United States on 15 October 2026, instructing candidates to request accommodations before scheduling.

As shown in the United States on 15 October 2026, the request pages reviewed for all 4 vendor policy routes say where to send requests or supporting files, but they do not determine whether one person's older documentation will be accepted. Review and arrangement windows describe different stages, not figures to add for a guaranteed date.

37 public leaderboard strategies, one screen: the arithmetic holds, the concentration does not

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I parsed every strategy on a public leaderboard this week - 37 ids from Collective2's server-rendered page, monthly tables only, no login - and recomputed each one locally before looking at any ranking.

What I recomputed: yearly monthly cells compounded against the printed YTD; annualised return as eq^(365/days)-1; the share of total log return carried by the best three months; annual return against reported max drawdown; record length; win rate against the break-even win rate implied by average win and average loss.

The arithmetic reconciles. Across 36 active strategies, every year's monthly cells compound to the printed YTD within 0.4pp, and the annual figure matches calendar-day annualisation within -1.6 to +0.2pp on the five strategies I checked by hand. That rules out "the platform cannot compute". Any criticism has to be about what the number means, not whether it adds up.

The limits, since a screen is not an audit: the leaderboard is a selection - it shows winners, and the strategies that disappeared are not measured; everything is hypothetical per the platform's own disclaimer; no broker statements were seen; the edge test assumes a fixed average win and loss.

One page with the table and both examples: https://github.com/rytjy/backtest-audit-kit/raw/main/docs/c2-screen-37.pdf Parsed table: https://github.com/rytjy/backtest-audit-kit/raw/main/docs/c2-screen-37.table.txt Repo: https://github.com/rytjy/backtest-audit-kit

Hugging Face’s 70k-Word MoE Handbook After 800k GPU Hours — Why Sparsity Still Wins for Local Real-Time Tools

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On 5 November 2026 Thomas Wolf and the Hugging Face team published The Ultimate MoE Training Handbook. It is roughly 70,000 words, 55 interactive figures, and runnable code, distilled from their own experiments that burned on the order of 800,000 GPU hours on the Alps cluster. The point of the handbook is not another theoretical survey. It is the operational answers teams actually hit: how many experts, what router, why half a step disappears into all-to-all, why a run can silently become 25× slower with clean logs, aux loss versus DeepSeek-style loss-free bias, and whether you can serve with fewer experts than you trained with.

Eighteen of the twenty highest-scoring open models on the Artificial Analysis index are MoEs. Dense is not dead, but the density that matters for local deployment is the active parameter count, not the total. Flint from Recursive is the recent public example: 39.8 B total, ~2.5 B active, Apache 2.0, claimed 125+ tok/s on an M4 Pro at short context once quantized. The handbook’s diagnostic sections explain the training side of why that ratio is achievable and stable.

From the public summaries and the handbook’s own framing:

These are the same constraints that show up when you try to run a local model inside a Windows process that is already paying the WASAPI capture tax, the audio engine mix, and the overlay draw.

A desktop tool that does live transcription, translation, or short reply generation has a hard latency budget measured from speech end to on-screen text. Cloud STT endpoints (Soniox RT v5 at ~47 ms time-to-final on recent Coval numbers, Qwen3-ASR 1.7B variants around 35 ms) look fast until you add the Windows capture path: device buffer, shared-mode mixing, communications enhancements, and your own client buffer. 20–40 ms of capture jitter is common; lower is fragile under load.

Local models help only if they fit and finish inside the remaining budget. MoE sparsity is the practical lever:

The handbook’s emphasis on router precision and load balance is relevant here too. A router that collapses to one expert under production traffic destroys the sparsity you counted on. The same “measure one flag at a time” methodology they used for training applies when you quantize and profile the inference path.

The MoE handbook does not solve the Windows capture problem. It does make the model side less of a mystery. If half the training step was communication, you already know that the inference router and expert dispatch will also be sensitive to memory layout and quantization. Measure it.

Keep the router in higher precision even if the experts are Q4. Log expert utilization. If one expert is taking 90 % of tokens, you paid for density you are not using.

On the capture side the equivalent is buffer sizing and mode selection. A 10 ms buffer looks great until the scheduler hiccups; 20–30 ms is the range that usually survives real meetings.

GPU passthrough and live CUDA snapshots remain hard (Agent Substrate + gVisor explicitly call this out). For pure CPU or integrated GPU paths the MoE density story is more useful today. Windows still owns the end-to-end number for any tool that has to listen to a call without injecting a bot.

The handbook is free, long, and grounded in measured runs. Read the sections on all-to-all and router precision first if you care about shipping something that has to finish inside a real-time budget instead of a leaderboard.

I'm building Talkaside, a Windows app for job interviews and calls in another language. Drafted with AI assistance from this project's code and logs.

JJ Gabriel reveals key motivation behind Real Madrid transfer after Man Utd exit

📅 2026-11-07  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

JJ Gabriel has revealed the key motivation behind his impending move to Real Madrid after turning his back on Manchester United.

Gabriel, who turned 16 on October 6, informed United of his desire to leave in September and has not trained with the club since then.

The young forward has attracted interest from several clubs across Europe, but has now reached a verbal agreement to join Real Madrid.

The forward joined United’s academy four years ago and quickly established himself as one of the most highly-rated youngsters at the club, featuring for the U18 side at the age of just 14.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Gabriel played under Michael Carrick during the club’s pre-season camp, but his entourage are understood to have grown unhappy with his first-team integration and playing opportunities at United.

The teenager, who has an Irish passport, could finalise a move to the Spanish giants as early as January next year, with United reportedly set to receive £350,000 in compensation.

And in a brief interview with the Spanish outlet Marca, Gabriel revealed he has one particular goal in mind for his time at Real.

‘My dream is to win a Ballon d’Or,’ he said.

When asked about a potential move to Barceolan instead, Gabriel simply responded: ‘Obviously, Madrid.’

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

A move to Real is a bold step at such a young age, and United legend Ryan Giggs believes Gabriel may have been better served following the example of Max Dowman, whose development is not being rushed by Arsenal or Mikel Arteta.

📰🔜 JJ Gabriel with Marca’s front page confirming the move to Real Madrid is close: “Can I keep this [newspaper] with me?”. pic.twitter.com/cerdxXsbE4

‘So far in his career everything is going fine, why would you disrupt that? You’re on the right track,’ Giggs said of Gabriel on Rio Ferdinand Presents.

‘I trained at 15, I didn’t play for the first team until two years later so you have to have that patience that people just don’t seem to have at the moment.

‘And believe it or not, first-team football for Man United is completely different to playing in the youth team, playing pre-season for the first team, it’s completely different. That first game, yeah, you might do alright, but you have to do it for 40, 50 games a season. You’re going to get injuries, loss of form, bad results, so you have to be patient.

‘If you’re on the right trajectory then just bide your time, no matter how good you are.

‘Max Dowman is unbelievable but he’s not playing for Arsenal at the moment.

‘I’m sure he’ll bide his time, I’m sure he’s getting impatient, but he’s not kicking off, he knows in a couple of years time he might be the main man for Arsenal.’

‘Triplets of Belleville’ Director Sylvain Chomet Fires Back at AI Accusations Over Dior Film Featuring Robert Pattinson’s Voice: ‘Everything Was Drawn by Hand’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

French animation filmmaker Sylvain Chomet, the four-time Oscar nominee behind “The Triplets of Belleville” and “The Illusionist,” is pushing back against accusations that generative AI was used to create his animated short for Dior featuring a voice cast led by Robert Pattinson, Rihanna, Josh O’Connor, LaKeith Stanfield, Louis Garrel and Camille Cottin.

The whimsical six-minute film, “Christian Dior’s Night in Color,” produced by Aton Soumache (“The Little Prince”), has sparked controversy online, where some viewers have pointed to apparent visual glitches, including a character with six fingers, as evidence of AI-generated animation. Related Stories 'Beef' Creator Lee Sung Jin Sets Netflix Projects With Ali Wong, Gene Hong Under New Banner Lila Pictures Netflix Names Mattel Executive Roberto Stanichi as VP of Consumer Products and Experiences

The controversy flared because Dior positioned “Night in Color” as a celebration of traditional craftsmanship to launch its 2026 holiday season, drawing parallels between Chomet’s hand-drawn animation and the meticulous work of the house’s couture ateliers.

But Chomet and Soumache tell Variety that the film was created using traditional 2D animation techniques, with the characters, backgrounds and key poses drawn by artists. While they acknowledge using automated interpolation to generate some of the intermediate frames that make movement fluid, the pair insists that generative AI was never used to create the artwork.

“Some people are claiming Sylvain was contacted two weeks ago and that the entire film was made with generative AI, without a single drawing, but the reality is that this film took seven months to make and it was entirely drawn by artists,” says Soumache, adding that film employed roughly 20 people, including six animators, alongside storyboard artists, designers, background artists, compositors and editors.

“We used an automated interpolation system for a very small part of the process, but the film itself is traditional 2D animation,” says the French producer.

Chomet, whose latest film “A Magnificent Life” world premiered at last year’s Cannes and was distributed theatrically by Sony Pictures Classics, explains that the interpolation tool was only used to help create the in-between frames on “Christian Dior’s Night in Color.” “It’s a way of automating the repetitive, mechanical part of animation, not the creative work,” he says.

The filmmaker says the accusations are particularly ironic given how committed he has been to traditional animation despite pressures to branch into CGI after the huge success of “Toy Story.”

“Before I made ‘The Triplets of Belleville,’ it was the era of ‘Toy Story.’ I remember Disney declaring that traditional 2D animation was dead and that everything would be CGI, but I decided to continue making hand-drawn films,” Chomet says. “That’s why I find it so strange that people are attacking me today. I’ve remained faithful to drawing throughout my career. At the same time, I’m going to use the tools that technology offers us.”

The film draws on several chapters of Dior’s history, including the couturier’s friendship with Marlene Dietrich, his ties to Hollywood and the creation of the iconic Junon gown in 1949. It also takes inspiration from one of the earliest ideas behind Dior creative director Jonathan Anderson’s Cruise 2027 collection: cinema’s transition from black-and-white to Technicolor.

The story imagines Dietrich in Los Angeles, preparing to shoot a movie when the director suddenly decides to film in color. An urgent call is placed to Paris requesting a colorful Dior gown for the actress, sending Christian Dior and his team of seamstresses into a race against the clock.

Pattinson narrates the film, while Garrel voices Christian Dior and Cottin plays Dietrich. O’Connor voices a talking chair, Rihanna portrays Dior’s lucky star, and Stanfield plays the Hollywood director. Nine d’Urso, Nadia Tereszkiewicz and Greta Lee voice the three bees.

Soumache, whose credits include the Cesar-winning “The Little Prince,” has spent much of his career working in CGI before returning to traditional animation with films including Joann Sfar’s “Little Vampire” and more recently “A Magnificent Life,” a biopic of late celebrated French novelist and filmmaker Marcel Pagnol.

“Sylvain and I have been championing traditional 2D animation for such a long time, and Dior came to us because they wanted Sylvain’s very distinctive drawing style,” says the producer whose track record also includes the blockbuster French IP “Miraculous Ladybug.”

Chomet explains that the production process on the Dior short was rooted in drawing, even if artists now use digital tablets rather than paper.

“But we’re still drawing by hand, it’s just done digitally,” he said, adding that he found it frustrating to work on graphic tablets and planned to “go back to pencil and paper because (he loves) having original drawings.”

“I design the characters, we create the storyboards, the backgrounds, the colors. The entire creative process is exactly the same as when I started making animated films. What has changed is the technology,” he said.

Chomet also addresses the apparent six-fingered hands that have fueled speculation online. “It’s a movement where the hand is spread out like a fan. In that kind of drawing, you might see six or seven fingers because you’re trying to convey movement,” he says. He argues that he’s seen much worse mistakes in traditional animation. “I’ve seen a character turn his head and suddenly his nose changes shape completely over five frames. These things happen.”

The short was conceived as part of a broader collaboration with Dior, with Anderson originating the idea and Martin Latham helping develop the screenplay.

“I worked particularly closely with Martin. We developed the script together and then presented ideas for the storyboard, the story and the characters. There was a lot of back-and-forth,” Chomet says. The collaboration also extended beyond the screen, with Chomet’s illustrations inspiring Dior’s holiday collections and boutique windows around the world.

“Dior needed to see the designs quite early because they were simultaneously developing the Christmas window displays and physical objects,” says Soumache. “Many of Dior’s windows are based directly on Sylvain’s drawings. They created physical versions of the characters and objects from the film.”

Chomet describes Disney’s 1961 classic “One Hundred and One Dalmatians” as his “madeleine de Proust,” the film he keeps coming back to.

“Whenever I make a film, I ask myself whether it has that poetry, that beauty of drawing,” he says. “We can tell very different stories from the ones Disney was telling at the time, but we can still preserve that beauty. That’s what I’ve always tried to do, using technology to maintain something traditional.”

Along with showcasing France’s vibrant animation talent pool, the short film also reflects Anderson’s love for hand-drawn animation. While at Loewe, he collaborated with Japan’s Studio Ghibli on three collections.

Inter-Como, sfida anche nei bilanci: sostenibilità contro investimenti

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Non soltanto una sfida sul campo. Inter e Como, avversarie domenica a San Siro, rappresentano anche due modelli economici differenti: da una parte una società storica impegnata nel risanamento dei conti, dall'altra un progetto ambizioso sostenuto da importanti investimenti. Un confronto tra sostenibilità e crescita, come evidenzia l'analisi della Gazzetta dello Sport.

Il club nerazzurro ha chiuso l'esercizio 2024-2025 con un utile di 35,4 milioni di euro, dopo due stagioni consecutive in perdita. Una svolta importante, considerando le difficoltà degli anni precedenti: nel 2020-2021 il rosso aveva raggiunto i 245 milioni, mentre le perdite cumulate in tre esercizi erano arrivate a circa 395 milioni. La crescita dei ricavi, superiori ai 560 milioni, e una maggiore attenzione alla gestione dei costi hanno favorito il cambio di rotta, anche grazie ai risultati sportivi e allo sviluppo commerciale.

Il percorso del Como è differente. Nel 2024-2025 il club ha registrato perdite per circa 130 milioni di euro, con ricavi intorno ai 62 milioni e un costo del personale di circa 55 milioni. Numeri che raccontano una strategia orientata a crescere rapidamente, rafforzando la rosa di Cesc Fabregas e investendo anche nelle infrastrutture e nell'area commerciale. Due filosofie diverse, dunque: l'Inter punta a mantenere competitività e stabilità finanziaria, mentre il Como investe oggi per costruire risultati e ricavi futuri. Domenica il confronto sarà sul campo, ma la partita tra i due modelli è già cominciata.

“We lost on that occasion and we'd like to put that right,” he said. “But I think, regardless of who we're playing against, we've had a great home record, scoring 26 goals from nine games.

Harrogate and Fylde sit second and fourth in the fifth-tier table and both favour a front-footed approach. The FA Cup meeting between them, therefore, could be quite the spectacle.

“I think both teams commit to a certain style of play,” said Weaver. “So there's that familiarity of both individuals and the style of play.

“Perhaps that incident happened a bit too early to be given. I don't know, but we were proud of the performance, not the outcome, but proud of the performance that night.”

Whoopi Goldberg Officially Returns As Guinan In Star Trek: Federation Fleet

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Guinan has long been one of Star Trek's most beloved supporting characters, with Whoopi Goldberg's portrayal of the mysterious El-Aurian earning her a special place among longtime fans. First appearing in Star Trek: The Next Generation in 1988, Guinan served as the bartender aboard the Enterprise-D, though her role quickly evolved into something far more important. Her extraordinary wisdom, mysterious past, and close friendship with Captain Jean-Luc Picard made her a true standout.

However, Guinan was never just an ordinary bartender. Having lived for centuries, she possessed remarkable knowledge and unusual abilities, including a heightened awareness of changes to the timeline. She's even one of the few characters capable of standing up to the omnipotent Q.

Screen Rant has previously ranked Guinan among the most powerful characters in the entire franchise, as well as one of its ten smartest, cementing her reputation as a truly formidable side character. Her enduring popularity eventually led to Goldberg reprising the role decades later in Star Trek: Picard, but now Whoopi is back again as Guinan in the upcoming release of Star Trek: Federation Fleet.

Whoopi Goldberg's Guinan is officially returning to the Star Trek franchise once again, this time as part of the massive Magic: The Gathering x Star Trek crossover launching on November 13, 2026. The beloved character is featured in the Federation Fleet Commander deck, which celebrates some of Starfleet's most recognizable characters, particularly those connected to Captain Jean-Luc Picard.

The new card, "Guinan, Ancient Advisor," features artwork by Nicky Barkla depicting Goldberg's iconic character. Classified as a rare Legendary Creature — El-Aurian Advisor, Guinan costs three mana to summon, including one white mana, and has a power/toughness rating of 1/4.

Her abilities are especially appropriate given Guinan's longstanding role as a trusted advisor aboard the Enterprise. Players can spend one mana and tap Guinan to place a +1/+1 counter on each creature they control that entered the battlefield during the current turn. If this results in at least two counters being distributed, the player also draws a card.

It's a fitting interpretation of Guinan's wisdom and her ability to strengthen those around her, and gives players a useful advantage, bringing multiple creatures into play during a single turn. The card even includes sage dialogue from the character in the flavor text:

“Human intuition and instinct are not always right, but they do make life interesting.”

Guinan is just one of several familiar faces featured in Federation Fleet, which is led by Jean-Luc Picard, Luminary. The 100-card Commander deck also includes characters such as Beverly Crusher, Captain Kathryn Janeway, and Miles O'Brien. Three other Commander decks are being released alongside Federation Fleet:

Each Commander deck contains 100 cards, including 32 new-to-Magic cards, and will be listed for roughly $75. Premium Collector's Editions are also available for about $160.

The larger crossover celebrates Star Trek's 60th anniversary with cards drawn from across the franchise's history, including both television shows and films. Pre-release events began on November 6, with the digital launch on MTG Arena coming up on November 10 and the full tabletop release on November 13, 2026.

Why are people sticking aluminum foil on their walls in winter? I asked the experts to explain exactly why this clever moisture trick works

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Here at Tom’s Guide our expert editors are committed to bringing you the best news, reviews and guides to help you stay informed and ahead of the curve!

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Sign up to get the latest updates on all of your favorite content! From cutting-edge tech news and the hottest streaming buzz to unbeatable deals on the best products and in-depth reviews, we’ve got you covered.

Be AI savvy with your weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editor and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

Unlock the vast world of Apple news straight to your inbox. With coverage on everything from exciting product launches to essential software updates, this is your go-to source for the latest updates on all the best Apple content.

Our weekly newsletter is expertly crafted to immerse you in the world of streaming. Stay updated on the latest releases and our top recommendations across your favorite streaming platforms.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Peeling paint, bubbling wallpaper, mold behind wardrobes, and water-stained ceilings — I’ve seen all the classic signs of excess moisture in my own home.

But while window condensation is an obvious telltale sign of too much moisture inside my house, the root cause of the other issues isn’t so clear.

But before calling in a specialist, there’s a simple test you can try to determine whether the moisture is coming from inside or out.

Beyond covering your Thanksgiving turkey, aluminum foil can help with a stack of hacks around your home, including identifying which sides of your walls are holding excess moisture.

All you need to do is take a piece of aluminum foil and tape it to your wall. Within 48 hours, it should give you an indication of where the moisture is coming from.

But how does this aluminum foil hack work, and is it reliable? Michael Rubino, mold and air quality expert at HomeCleanse, explains: “Aluminum foil doesn’t allow water vapor to pass through it easily, because it’s a non-porous surface. When you tape it against a wall, you’re essentially blocking moisture from moving between that section of the wall and the air in the room.”

And he adds, “If the air inside your home is humid and the foil gets cool enough, that moisture in the air can condense into water droplets on the side facing the room.”

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

The same process happens when you spot condensation on the inside of your windows.

However, what if the moisture is coming from the wall? Rubino explains, “If there is already moisture in the wall, that moisture would normally have some opportunity to evaporate into the room as the material dries. Covering it with foil, prevents that from happening in that particular spot. In this case, moisture collects behind the foil instead.”

Rubino explains that checking which side of the aluminum foil is wet can tell you something about how moisture is behaving in that exact area. But it can’t tell you exactly where that water came from.

If you remove the foil and find moisture against the wall, you might assume rainwater is getting in from outside, but he says that’s only one possibility. “A pipe could be leaking inside the wall, or water could be entering through the foundation.”

He explains that other possibilities include water entering through the foundation or condensation forming inside the wall cavity due to high humidity or poor insulation.

It's also possible you have more than one moisture issue at once. Rubino explains, “If you find water droplets on the side facing the room, that doesn’t mean you’ve ruled out a leak behind the wall, either.”

Dr. Alex Le Beau, a toxicologist and environmental scientist at Exposure Consulting, says a visual assessment is a good way to start checking whether moisture has entered your home, and professionals will do this too.

He explains that wet building material, such as a drywall, within the built environment is not normal, and that the location of the moisture should be the first thing to note — is it on an exterior or interior wall or the ceiling?

He also advises looking at the pattern it is making. Depending on the material, he says, "It will take a good amount of water to soak through the back of something. Soaking like this may make the material brittle to the touch or could cause surface deformations."

Dr. Le Beau says to look out for paint bubbling or staining coming through from the back of the material.

Internal condensation may also look different. He says, “There may be a pattern to the moisture from internal humidity, or it may form at the same spot and then evaporate when internal conditioning (HVAC) begins to cycle."

While a dry sheet of aluminum foil on your wall may seem like a good sign, Rubino warns against using a single piece of information to judge your home’s condition. "There’s too much it can’t account for, and a dry result doesn’t mean you’re in the clear,” he says.

Another of Rubino’s concerns about this hack is that it can’t be used to detect mold. "You could find moisture behind the foil and have no idea whether mold has started growing inside the wall,” he says.

It also won’t determine how much of the surrounding area is affected or whether contamination has spread beyond that area.

He’s also found that the opposite can be true. "I’ve seen plenty of situations where the original water problem happened months or even years earlier,” he says, “The material may have dried out since then, but that doesn't mean any mold growth that developed during that time has disappeared as well."

This GoveeLife dehumidifier is easy to use, super powerful, and can be controlled via an intuitive app. Our tester thought it was one of the best among dehumidifiers of this size, although it is noisy. It also has a dedicated 'Dry Mode' which can be used on clothing. Read the review

While the aluminum foil hack can give you an idea of what’s happening on your walls, it doesn’t give you a definitive answer. And that’s why Rubino says it’s important to understand the entire situation and to ask questions: where has the moisture come from, how long has it been there, what materials have been affected, and has microbial growth developed?

“A piece of foil isn't going to answer all those questions, and those are the answer you really need if you’re concerned about the health of your home," he explains.

And he adds, “If you notice discoloration around a window, a musty smell that keeps coming back, or an area that never seems to dry completely, don’t just paint over it or assume it’s nothing. Find out what’s causing it.”

The sooner you understand what’s going on, the better your chances of addressing the problem before the damage becomes more extensive.

Toei CEO Fumio Yoshimura Talks Global Expansion, GenAI Adoption & Entry Into Games

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fumio Yoshimura, CEO of Japan’s Toei, explained how the studio is working with local partners to globalize its long-established IP, as well as innovating in areas including GenAI and gaming, in a keynote Forum session at Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF).

In 2023, the 75-year-old studio launched an ambitious $2BN ‘Toei New Wave’ plan to boost its international revenue’s share of total income from 30% to 50% by 2033, which included upgrades to its studio facilities in Tokyo and Kyoto. Since then the company has embarked on several international co-productions and partnerships, started exploring GenAI technology and moved into production of games. Related Stories News 'Dune' Star Chang Chen Introduces Six Rising Taiwanese Actors In Golden Horse & TCCF Showcase News Weta FX CEO Daniel Seah Unpacks TAICCA Partnership: "Taiwan Has Creative Talent, Technology & Government Support"

Yoshimura started the session by explaining how the company has been introducing its popular IP to international markets. One of its oldest franchises, Kamen Rider, is currently streaming in Taiwan and other international markets, with India a particular focus. Watch on Deadline

“In 2025, we held an event to introduce Kamen Rider to India consumers. We got a very good response and signed a contract with a Disney affiliate to distribute the content. Due to its population size and the growth of its middle-class, we see India as a promising market,” Yoshimura said.

Kamen Rider was one of the first franchises that Toei globalized, working with U.S. partners to produce an English-language remake, Mighty Morphin Power Rangers, more than 30 years ago, with the franchise now handled by toymaker Hasbro. Both the Kamen Rider and Power Rangers franchises have been extended to toys, merchandising and live events.

In other IP extensions, Toei recently teamed with Sony’s Anyplex Inc and Godzilla VFX house Shriogumi Inc to launch a Kamen Rider anime label.

More recent localization efforts include the Voltes 5 franchise, which was first launched as an anime in Japan in 1977 and became a cultural phenomenon in the Philippines where its themes of resistance against a repressive empire got it banned by the Marcos regime. In 2023, Philippines broadcaster GMA produced a live-action remake, Voltes V: Legacy, which became a ratings hit in the Philippines and also aired in Japan.

“Simply exporting content limits its reach, so in order to break through constraints, we are producing remakes and localisations,” Yoshimura explained.

After getting started with remakes, Yoshimura said the studio is now also working on local productions based on original stories: “We’re developing local content from scratch, working with local talent to release locally and then sell globally. We believe this local first model will generate more sustainable long-term revenue than the traditional export model.”

Among recent productions, Toei worked with Taiwan’s Lou Lou Film Studio on Dear Stranger, directed by Tetsuya Mariko and starring Hidetoshi Nishijima and Gwei Lun-mei, which screened on the festival circuit last year. More recently, the company worked with Taiwan’s Wowing Entertainment Group on horror film The Rope Curse 4: Kuntilanak, directed by Liao Shih-Han and starring Lee Kang-sheng.

Yoshimura identified Taiwan as another key market for Toei’s global expansion, due to cultural similarities and the popularity of Japanese IP in Taiwan. “Data shows that Japanese films had a 26% share of Taiwan’s box office last year, the second highest share after Hollywood with 41%,” Yoshimura said. “Taiwanese audiences love Japanese content.”

Toei has also partnered with Thailand’s M Studio on a slate of Thai-language remakes and local features, and is currently working with both M Studio and Korea’s Showbox on a horror feature based on Japanese manga The Long Hair In The Attic, by artist Junji Ito.

The studio has also stepped into assisting the growing number of international productions now shooting in Japan, encouraged by Japan’s incentives scheme, including U.S. indie action thriller Iris, created by and starring Dani Swan (The Matrix Resurrections). In addition to providing facilities, Toei helped with casting for the film and assisted on the incentives application.

On the tech front, Yoshimura explained that the company is expanding the Virtual Production and motion capture facilities at its studios in Tokyo and Kyoto and also starting to use AI in workflows.

“We’re also experimenting with GenAI image generation,” Yoshimura said. “The Toei team is performing tests on practical AI applications daily; building tools to assist junior producers in estimating production budgets, and using AI in background artwork. However we believe human creativity remains the core of everything we do.”

Microdrama is another area that Toei has stepped into – producing Everyday Host Club, a 24×3 minutes anime series, and a 30-episode shortform vertical drama made for smartphone screens.

Gaming is another new area for Toei as it explores how to expand its IP across borders and formats. “Gaming is a global entertainment medium and delivering games to users worldwide aligns perfectly with our global expansion strategy,” Yoshimura said.

Toei plans to launch its first game, Hino produced by Ungloom Studio, in spring 2027, followed by Debug – Nephemee and Killa later next year. All the games will be distributed across platforms and consoles including Steam, Nintendo and Xbox.

The session entitled ‘Survival Strategies for Global Enterprises: Japan’s Toei on IP, Globalization, and Business Mindset’ took place as part of TCCF’s Forum section, which also includes sessions on Japan’s K2P Film Fund, Indonesian studio MD Entertainment’s scaling strategy, as well as panel discussions on co-production, mircodrama, AI adoption and IP exploitation.

TCCF is taking place November 6-9 at the Nangang Exhibition Center in Taipei.

11 most valuable EFL Championship players and how much they earn

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In recent years, the Championship has become a league that plays host to some incredibly valuable players, which now eclipse some of the top leagues across Europe.

Parachute payments and the money that comes from television rights means that top second tier clubs can keep most of their best players, and they can spend millions bolstering their squads each summer - figures are being spent that wouldn't have been dreamed of 20 years ago.

Here, FLW takes a look at the most valuable Championship starting 11 in a 4-2-3-1 formation, as per valuations made by Transfermarkt, and what they're all estimated to be earning in wages per week, with estimates taken from Capology.

A sufferer of back-to-back Premier League relegations with two different clubs, Mads Hermansen hasn't had too much joy in the last few years of his career.

The Dane was impressive enough with Leicester City to earn himself a £20 million move to West Ham last summer, but he spent half of his debut season at the London Stadium on the bench, albeit he ended it as Nuno's number one, and has been between the sticks throughout the current season.

With a market value of €15 million (£12.5m) and an estimated wage of £22,500-per week, the 26-year-old still holds value, but it's now below what was paid for his services last year.

The abilities of Jackson Tchatchoua are a bone of contention amongst Wolves fans, and he is now second-choice to Kieran Trippier when both are fully-fit and available.

The athletic Cameroon international arrived at Molineux last summer in a £10.8 million deal from Hellas Verona, where he was dubbed the fastest player in Serie A.

For all his speed though, Tchatchoua's performances have left a lot to be desired, and in the end he was part of last summer's recruitment drive which really didn't go down well on the whole.

Tchatchoua still holds a €12 million (£10m) market value though, which could see him be worth something in January if the club wanted to move him on, but is earning an estimated £65,000-a week in the Midlands, which could prove more problematic to shift.

He may not be the best at helping to keep goals out for West Ham at one end, but Konstantinos Mavropanos has certainly proved to be a goal threat in the Championship.

The Greek centre-half joined the Hammers for £19 million in 2023, but was part of a leaky defence last season that saw the Londoners drop into the Championship.

And whilst the defence hasn't been exactly watertight in the second tier, Mavropanos has been showing up at the other end of the pitch - at the time of writing, he's fired in five goals and has been showing up the club's strikers in a big way.

An €18 million (£15m) market valuation feels fair, with an estimated weekly wage of £25,000 feeling quite low, but there's likely going to be transfer interest in January - Besiktas are already being linked.

It was perhaps a surprise to some that Wolves managed to keep hold of Czechia centre-back Ladislav Krejci in the summer, but they're able to utilise him until at least January as a result.

Funnily enough, West Ham made a push to sign him late on in the transfer window, but with Wolves not wanting to sell to a divisional rival, and the player not wanting to play in the Championship, the deal was off - yet he remains in that league anyway.

Wolves had to pay £26 million for the centre-back from Girona after an appearance-based clause was triggered in his loan deal, and whilst he's an obvious good player for the Championship, a market value of €22 million (£18.3m) seems about right.

It would mean Wolves would make a financial loss if selling him for that price - he also comes with a chunky estimated weekly wage of £45,000.

West Ham youngster Ollie Scarles is the most valuable left-back in the division, according to Transfermarkt, at €12 million (£10m) - yet he's not even a regular starter at the London Stadium.

A Hammers academy product, Scarles played 13 times in the Premier League last season, and was expected to take big steps forward in the Championship under Nuno.

The 20-year-old did start the campaign in the starting 11, but has seen his game-time dwindle thanks to the presence of Nottingham Forest loanee Morato.

There's still time for Scarles to make an impact though this season, and given the home-grown rules for the Championship, he's unlikely to be leaving anytime soon, and with an estimated wage of £10,000-a week, he's one of the lower earners at the club.

On the other end of the spectrum, West Ham summer signing Arne Engels is estimated to earn a cool £50,000-per week - which is to be expected given his £22 million move from Celtic broke the Championship transfer record.

Has the Belgium international made enough of an impact to be worthy of that fee so far though? It's hard to say - he scored his first goal for the club in a 3-3 comeback draw against Cardiff City, and he's also notched a handful of assists, but he's not been a standout midfielder that looks far and away Premier League quality.

Scott Parker may be able to unlock another level to Engels' game, and with an estimated valuation of €25 million (£20.8m), there surely has to be more to come.

Wolves broke the bank in 2024 when signing Brazilian midfielder André from Fluminense for £21 million, but he probably didn't envisage playing in the Championship just two years later.

A tough-tackling midfield general who also possesses technique, it would've been a fair assumption that André would've signed for a top flight club over the summer, but to the surprise of many, he signed a brand-new contract at Molineux.

Like Engels, André's market valuation of €25 million (£20.8m) is in the right ballpark, and that will likely increase if Wolves win promotion back to the Premier League at the first time of asking - an estimated wage of £65,000-a week for the Championship though is a really steep one.

If anyone deserved a move back to the Premier League this summer after a relegation on his CV, it was Jarrod Bowen, who has served West Ham remarkably well for nearly seven years.

Bowen's nine goals in 2025-26 weren't enough to help keep the Hammers in the Premier League, but he committed his immediate future to the club in the wake of relegation, albeit there is a release clause that can be triggered next summer if they do not win promotion at the first time of asking.

Currently, Bowen is showing exactly what he's capable of at a lower level, but for an estimated £75,000-a week and a €30 million (£25m) transfer valuation, he's doing what is expected, and he's probably capable of even better - West Ham won't be entertaining selling him in January, but they might not have a choice in the summer.

After struggling to make an impact in the Premier League last season following his £19 million move from Celta Vigo, Fer Lopez has returned a different player.

Returning to Celta on loan for the second half of 2025-26 may have helped the Spaniard's confidence a bit, as he's now ripping the Championship apart with his flair and attacking nous, becoming the focal point of the Wolves attack under Cesar Peixoto.

He's looking every inch of the player that is estimated to have a transfer value of €22 million (18.3m), and he's probably worth more than that - his estimated wage of £25,000-a week is on the low side as well for Wolves' first-team squad.

Wolves didn't have much to cheer about last season, but the emergence of Mateus Mane in the first-team was one of the bright sparks.

Mane ended up playing 27 times for Wolves in the Premier League, and despite a number of top flight clubs across Europe apparently being keen on the Portugal youth international in the summer, he remained in the West Midlands.

FLW has consulted AI chatbot ChatGPT to name the best eight owners in the Championship.

Estimated to be on £7,000-a week at Molineux from a contract signed in the September 2025, which puts him on their books until 2029, an estimated transfer value of €23 million (£19.2m) is reasonable for a player of his potential - even if he hasn't lit up the Championship as expected.

The 19-year-old may just need more time to mature and develop, but when all the pieces get put together, there could be a really exciting player that is back in the Premier League very soon.
