Jensen Huang Says AI Needs 1,000 Times More Power. Here's Why Oklo and NuScale Power May Still Suffer.

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Artificial intelligence remains desperate for more energy sources.

Despite a recent pullback, artificial intelligence (AI) stocks remain some of the market's best performers in recent years. And projections look very positive for additional AI spending and continued rapid adoption. Goldman Sachs now forecasts AI spending to surpass $1 trillion in 2026. Meanwhile, more than one-quarter of all U.S. businesses are now regularly using artificial intelligence products.

The recent pullback in AI stocks wasn't driven by uncertainty about demand. Instead, there are rising concerns regarding the pace of the global data center build-out. To scale adoption, more data centers will need to be built. Doing so, however, generally requires support from the communities these facilities will be located in -- support that is increasingly difficult to find. Plus, these facilities require substantial capital to build. The complete availability of that capital is currently in question.

"AI demand remains strong," affirms recent research from Barclays. "What the market is questioning is funding, as hyperscalers keep lifting capex while free cash flow evaporates."

Concerns about capital availability and public support for additional data centers are relatively new but intensifying. What isn't new is the market's concern over how AI companies will power their data centers.

"Capital is pouring into data center development, but there are real constraints on growth," McKinsey & Co. warns. "Incumbents can't meet demand for power."

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang agrees. "The amount of energy that we need for [AI] computing is likely probably 1,000 times more than we currently have," he said earlier this year.

It is this need for more energy generation capacity that has driven enthusiasm for nuclear energy stocks, including Oklo (OKLO +22.66%) and NuScale Power (SMR +16.25%). Both of these companies are developing small modular reactors, or SMRs. These systems can, at least on paper, be built faster with lower initial capital requirements than conventional nuclear power plants. That makes them an ideal fit for the AI industry, which needs to scale as much new power generation as quickly as possible.

This investment thesis -- that SMR stocks will benefit from the AI industry's desperate need for more energy -- has driven much of the bullish sentiment around Oklo and NuScale Power. Recent developments, however, have called the strength of that thesis into question.

Despite industry hype, Bank of America's estimates don't predict real-world SMR adoption will scale meaningfully until the 2030s or even the 2040s. That's a problem for NuScale Power and Oklo, given that both companies remain unprofitable and the full commercialization of their SMR designs may still be years away.

To sustain themselves financially, both Oklo and NuScale Power have sold large blocks of shares to raise cash, diluting shareholders. With capital and social support concerns on the rise, SMR adoption by AI and data center companies could be delayed, especially since lower-cost, lower-risk alternatives such as solar, natural gas, and wind remain accessible.

This year, shares of Oklo and NuScale are both down more than 60%. If the SMR build-out is stretched out, more stock issuances will be on the way, this time at rock-bottom prices. While I still believe in the SMR story over the long term from a business perspective, the investment case for SMR stocks is currently facing mounting uncertainty.

Bank of America is an advertising partner of Motley Fool Money. Ryan Vanzo has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

I’ve spent months testing the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Galaxy S26 Ultra, and Pixel 11 Pro XL — here's the flagship I recommend

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This is the one that's worth I'd buy with my own money

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Flagship phones continue to be the most coveted devices on the market, prized for their top-tier specs, robust software suites, and class-leading cameras. Over the past year, I’ve spent months testing many of the best phones on the market — including the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Galaxy S26 Ultra, and Pixel 11 Pro XL.

Choosing between these heavyweights isn’t as simple as glancing at a spec sheet or trying them out for five minutes at a nearby carrier store. When you plan to shell out well over $1,299, you need to know how each holds up under daily use.

That’s why I’m pitting all three against each other to break down their key strengths, inevitable weaknesses, and most importantly, which flagship is truly worth your money.

6.9-inch Super Retina XDR display (2,868 x 1,320)

6.9-inch Dynamic AMOLED 2X (3,120 x 1,440)

6.8-inch 1,344 x 2,992 Super Actua (OLED)

48MP main with variable aperture (f/1.48, f/1.8, f/2.8, f/4), 48MP ultrawide (f/2.2), 48MP telephoto w/4x optical zoom (f/2.8)

200MP main (f/1.4), 50MP ultrawide (f/1.9), 10MP 3x telephoto (f/2.4), 50MP 5x telephoto (f/2.9)

50MP (f/1.68) main, 48MP (f/1.7) ultrawide, 48MP (f/2.8) 5x telephoto

Black, White, Sky Blue, Cobalt Violet, Silver Shadow, Pink Gold

Even though it’s the one I’ve spent the least amount of time with, the iPhone 18 Pro Max is a marked improvement over the iPhone 17 Pro Max. Apple didn’t hold back here because out of the dozens of devices I’ve tested this year alone, it earns its title as the best camera phone on the market for several reasons.

First and foremost, the main camera features a mechanical variable aperture lens that provides genuine creative control — some examples of which you can see in the gallery above. Wide open, it yields natural optical bokeh, while stopped down, it captures razor-sharp shots edge to edge.

Apple also finally introduced native manual controls courtesy of Pro Controls — a feature notably absent even on the pricier iPhone Duo. This unlocks granular adjustments for shutter speed, aperture, white balance, manual focus, and ISO without forcing you into third-party camera apps. Better yet, these same manual controls extend directly to video recording.

In low light, the iPhone 18 Pro Max is virtually unmatched. While most modern flagships deliver comparable dynamic range under direct midday sun, Apple pulls ahead once the sun dips below the horizon, as you can see in the nighttime sample shots I shot above.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Beyond imaging, the phone is a powerhouse driven by the 2nm A20 Pro chip. Our benchmark testing confirms it sits a level above its rivals in raw computing and graphics processing, yet it remains exceptionally power-efficient — lasting a staggering 18 hours and 27 minutes on our custom battery test to outlast its chief competitors.

Software remains the only mixed bag. While iOS 27 adds welcome Apple Intelligence features — including personal favorites like Reframe — the platform still lags behind the competition in its suite of AI features. Siri AI feels like a breath of fresh air compared to previous generations, but it still falls short of Google’s Gemini. In daily testing, its limited integration with third-party apps keeps it from feeling truly indispensable.

The Galaxy S26 Ultra continues to be the undisputed Swiss Army knife of smartphones, with this year’s model bringing several notable hardware upgrades. It’s also the flagship I’ve spent the most time testing, having pitted it extensively against both its predecessors and its fiercest rivals.

One of the standout hardware innovations this year is the native Privacy Display, an exclusive feature unlike anything else on the market. It delivers immediate peace of mind by shielding the screen from prying eyes and shoulder surfers — a constant nuisance during my daily subway commute.

On other devices, I’m forced to install privacy screen protectors that permanently degrade screen brightness and color accuracy. With the S26 Ultra, I can toggle the effect on or off at will.

While the S26 Ultra came up just short of the iPhone 18 Pro Max in my camera face-off, it remains vastly superior in one crucial area: telephoto reach. Samsung’s upgraded periscope telephoto lens doesn't just make distance shooting effortless; its combination of optical sharpness and sensor stabilization allowed me to read the minuscule parking regulations on a street sign from clear across the avenue in our sample shots above.

Productivity and software versatility are where the S26 Ultra builds an insurmountable lead. While Samsung’s underlying Google Gemini integration shares parity with other premier Android flagships, it proves just how transformative mobile AI can be when properly integrated. Gemini’s Task Automation genuinely impresses with its ability to execute complex, multi-step actions across disparate apps. Case in point: I was able to place a Dunkin Donuts order through DoorDash with a voice prompt, and it arrived at my door 30 minutes later.

Then there’s the integrated S Pen, an enduring staple you simply won’t find on rival flagships. It remains an unmatched precision tool for annotating documents, sketching concepts, and signing contracts on the go. Even if you don’t pull it out daily, having a built-in active stylus ready without paying extra for an accessory or specialized case adds tremendous value.

Finally, there is the ultimate fallback whenever I accidentally leave my laptop behind: Samsung DeX. Nothing matches the desktop workflow DeX enables when I tether the S26 Ultra to an external monitor or a pair of smart glasses. It transforms the phone into an actual workstation, proving once and for all that I have a full computer sitting in my pocket.

For anyone who prioritizes software above all else, the Pixel 11 Pro XL is the clear contender — and it continues to defend its title as the best AI phone on the market. I’m not just talking about staples like its stock Android 17 interface or Google’s industry-leading pledge of seven years of major OS updates and Pixel Feature Drops.

While it shares baseline Gemini AI features with other Android devices, Google bakes in exclusive software that makes the overall experience feel far more robust. Standouts include Proactive Assistance, which intelligently surfaces contextual actions based on my on-screen activity; Gboard’s Rambler, which offers unmatched conversational voice dictation; and the Creator Studio suite, complete with a built-in teleprompter for mobile video creators. These newcomers sit on top of proven Pixel essentials like Call Screen, Voice Translate, and Pro Res Zoom.

Speaking of zoom, the Pixel 11 Pro XL delivers the longest digital reach in the lineup’s history, maxing out at 120x magnification. When paired with Pro Res Zoom, computational upscaling reconstructs distant details and cleans up grain with remarkable precision — hold it up against the Galaxy S26 Ultra in our sample shots above to see the difference. Creator-focused additions like Magic Capture and custom Camera Looks add even more versatility to an already formidable imaging setup.

The hardware also earns major praise for its 6.8-inch Super Actua display. In our lab testing, it reclaimed the brightness crown, hitting an astonishing 2,877 nits of peak brightness. Direct midday sun simply doesn't wash it out.

The trade-offs, however, stem from familiar hardware limitations. Google's Tensor G6 chip continues to trail Apple and Samsung by noticeable margins across synthetic CPU and GPU benchmarks, which trickles down into less efficient power draw and noticeably shorter battery endurance. Meanwhile, the new rear-mounted HiLight LED feels more like Google’s latest hardware gimmick than a practical tool — reminiscent of the temperature sensor of generations past.

Choosing between the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Galaxy S26 Ultra, and Pixel 11 Pro XL is a much easier call now that all three share the exact same $1,299 starting price. Samsung’s Ultra has traditionally been the most expensive of the bunch, but that’s no longer the case — which makes my final verdict much clearer.

If I were spending my own money today, the Galaxy S26 Ultra is the flagship I’d buy. Even though the iPhone 18 Pro Max remains an absolute beast with its record-setting battery endurance and superb camera system, the Galaxy S26 Ultra simply delivers the most versatile package for the price. Between its class-leading periscope zoom, the desktop productivity powerhouse of Samsung DeX, Gemini-powered task automation, the integrated S Pen, and the sheer everyday practicality of the Privacy Display, it does things neither rival can match.

When you’re dropping $1,299 on a smartphone, you shouldn't be forced to compromise on utility. Samsung packs the S26 Ultra with everything I’d want in my pocket to tackle my workday and my daily commute.

Paraguay's president impeached and under house arrest after bank collapse

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Late night political drama as PeÃ±a is impeached and arrested

Paraguay's President Santiago PeÃ±a has been impeached and placed under house arrest after a bank collapse triggered a crisis that left more than half of the country's population without access to their accounts.

PeÃ±a was arrested on Friday minutes after lawmakers, including from his own party, unanimously voted to remove him from office following the collapse earlier this week of Ueno Bank, which has links to his private business interests.

He had offered to step down on Thursday. Rather than accept his resignation, senators impeached PeÃ±a and charged him with misconduct, meaning he has been stripped of the immunity usually granted to ex-presidents.

PeÃ±a, who until last year held shares in the company that owned the bank, has denied any wrongdoing.

The impeachment charges that led to PeÃ±a's removal alleged he had conflicts of interest, manipulated regulatory oversight, diverted pension funds, and participated in an institutional cover-up involving the central bank.

He is due to be questioned on Saturday over allegations of breach of trust, influence peddling, and misappropriation of funds, prosecutor Silvia Margarita Gonzalez Vester said.

PeÃ±a's deputy, Pedro Alliana, has been sworn in as the new interim president and promised not to "abandon" the millions of people affected by the scandal.

"Compatriots who are now going through a difficult time as a result of the financial collapse will be fully compensated," said Alliana, who will serve as president until 2028.

The demise of the digital bank left more than half of Paraguay's population without access to their savings after it was seized by Paraguay's central bank.

Long lines of people trying to withdraw money from Ueno branches snaked down the streets of the capital Asuncion on Tuesday and Wednesday.

An estimated three million depositors remain unable to access their savings and pensions, prompting public outrage particularly in the capital, AsunciÃ³n.

Protests erupted across the country in response, with thousands taking to the streets on Thursday to demand their money.

Earlier demonstrations featured clashes with the police, who used tear gas and water cannons on the crowd.

PeÃ±a's impeachment passed with 44 out of 45 senators voting in favour of it. One lawmaker was absent.

"It was never my intention to betray the trust you placed in me," PeÃ±a said in a video posted on X announcing his resignation, adding his priority was "the peace of mind of Paraguayans who entrusted their savings to our financial system".

Afterwards, senate president Basilio NÃºÃ±ez said PeÃ±a was found guilty of "dereliction of duty".

Pedro Alliana takes the oath of office as interim President

Senator Rafael Filizzola said: "We are facing the biggest corruption scandal in our democratic history â a tremendous blow to the state, to private citizens, and to the country's credibility and image."

Analysts say PeÃ±a is likely to now be prosecuted over the bank's collapse.

His defence lawyer Ricardo Preda described the allegations as a "reckless accusation" that was "irresponsible and opportunistic".

Not playing at Man City 'horrendous' - Trafford

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

'I had to play regularly for my mental wellbeing' - Trafford

James Trafford says not playing for Manchester City last season was "horrendous" before he moved to Leeds United in the summer in search of first-team football.

City re-signed academy graduate Trafford in July 2025, two years after he had left the club to join Championship side Burnley.

But less than six weeks after his arrival, Italy international Gianluigi Donnarumma arrived from Paris St-Germain.

The 27-year-old, who helped the French club win the Champions League just weeks earlier, immediately became City's first-choice goalkeeper, leaving Trafford short of playing time.

After starting the first three matches of the campaign, 24-year-old Trafford made just one further Premier League start in the 35 games that followed Donnarumma's arrival.

Speaking on Football Daily's The Football Interview, Trafford reflected on the difficulties he encountered during a season on the fringes of Pep Guardiola's squad.

"What a horrendous, horrendous feeling. Not playing," said Trafford.

"You are ringing a lot of people and just venting. Like most people, you have a [bad] day at work and you ring someone up.

"I ring my dad, I ring my mum, I ring my missus, I ring my mates. I like ringing my mates and we can just have a good talk about it all. It's quite funny."

Despite his limited playing time, Trafford believes he still gained plenty from his final season at City, having helped the club win the League Cup and FA Cup after starting both finals at Wembley.

"Going into any season, I always have the highest of hopes and always the belief that it will go the best way it could," he added.

"Unfortunately, we didn't win all four trophies. It didn't go how I expected it to go, but it happens.

"In the moment, I just tried to make the best of it as I could. Looking back, I don't think it was a disappointing season in the end because I learned so much, I improved so much and I won two trophies in one year.

"There were moments when I was very down, regretting things and feeling disappointed, but looking back, I'm very thankful for the experience. What an incredible year."

Trafford was part of England's squad for the 2026 World Cup and, although he did not get game time, being part of Thomas Tuchel's squad was an "incredible experience" that he says he will not forget.

"I didn't play, but it is what it is," said Trafford. "I didn't expect to play when I got called up. I knew what my role was, but you just take as much experience as possible, learn from it and keep trying to win the day, because the more days you win, the better position you're in."

Next time around, though, Trafford hopes to be England's starting goalkeeper and believes that if he continues his impressive performances with Leeds, that ambition is entirely achievable.

"It's my aim and it's something I've always dreamed of," said Trafford.

"I always believe that one day I could be. Every day I try to keep improving to put myself in the best position to be England's number one one day.

"I think everyone in the goalkeeping department with the national team wants to be number one because it's an amazing position to be in."

'Everyone's got an opinion on goalkeeping'

After returning from the World Cup, Trafford left City to join Leeds United in a deal worth up to Â£45m - a record for a British goalkeeper.

Since arriving at Elland Road, he has relished the opportunity to play regularly in the Premier League.

The former Bolton Wanderers and Accrington Stanley loanee has started all nine of Leeds' Premier League matches this season, keeping three clean sheets, with only Brighton (seven) and Arsenal (five) having conceded fewer goals than Daniel Farke's side (eight).

His performances have helped Leeds to 12th in the table before this weekend's fixtures - just two points off the European places - and he says the club are only going to get better.

"I just think it's an incredible club," he said. "You look at the fanbase, everyone here - it's an incredible place. It's just somewhere that's only going and getting better.

"We'd love to get back into Europe. I think every club you ask would like to get into Europe, so the main goal is just to keep on improving."

Inter-Como verso il sold out: ultimi biglietti, live show e le info per i tifosi a San Siro

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Inter-Como verso il sold out: ultimi biglietti, live show e le info per i tifosi a San Siro

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Dopo il successo in Champions League contro il Feyenoord, l'Inter ritrova subito San Siro: i nerazzurri scendono in campo contro il Como nell'11ª giornata di Serie A. Si gioca domenica 8 novembre alle 18:00.

La sfida del Meazza chiuderà un periodo ricco di impegni per i nerazzurri: Inter-Como, ultima partita prima della sosta del campionato, sarà un appuntamento affascinante, al quale i tifosi interisti parteciperanno con il consueto entusiasmo: la partita viaggia spedita verso il sold out. Gli ultimi biglietti disponibili per Inter-Como sono disponibili su inter.it/tickets grazie alla rivendita dedicata agli Abbonati FULL.

Domenica 8 novembre sarà in vigore la Determinazione dell’ONMS 40/2026, per la quale i residenti in provincia di Como avranno la possibilità di acquistare solo i biglietti del settore ospiti.

Il pre-partita di San Siro sarà ancora una volta ricco di intrattenimento e momenti imperdibili con l’Inter Live Show presentato da Daniele Battaglia e Mirko Mengozzi. Lo show accompagnerà i tifosi dall’apertura dei cancelli fino al fischio d’inizio: sul palco si alterneranno giochi, musica con il DJ e tante attività pensate per coinvolgere il pubblico per rendere unica l’attesa verso Inter-Como. Sarà inoltre attivo il live entertainment su maxischermo tramite QR code: una nuova possibilità per coinvolgere maggiormente i tifosi, rendendoli parte attiva dello spettacolo di San Siro. Il pubblico potrà scattarsi fotografie da trasmettere al maxischermo, essere coinvolto con divertenti fan cam, scegliere le tracce musicali preferite per caricare la squadra e partecipare a speciali quiz.

Come di consueto si terrà anche il concorso dedicato ai tifosi nerazzurri, con in palio le maglie gara dell’Inter. Partecipare è semplice e gratuito: sarà possibile farlo entro un’ora dal calcio d’inizio, con la premiazione che avverrà direttamente sul palco a bordocampo, regalando ai vincitori un’esperienza ancora più speciale nel cuore del Meazza.

Tra le tante attività previste nel pre partita di Inter-Como, i tifosi potranno iniziare a vivere le emozioni della gara attraverso lo speciale Light Show powered by Enel, match sponsor della sfida di San Siro. Uno spettacolo imperdibile, che permetterà al pubblico nerazzurro di entrare fin da subito nel clima partita partecipando in prima persona: i tifosi sono chiamati a diventare protagonisti, prendendo parte attraverso le torce dei propri smartphone allo speciale Light Show powered by Enel. Per farlo basterà inquadrare i QR code che saranno trasmessi sui maxischermi dello stadio: tutti i tifosi interessati potranno anche partecipare cliccando su questo link.

L'apertura dei cancelli per Inter-Como è in programma alle ore 16:00. Considerato il grande afflusso previsto si raccomanda a tutti i tifosi di presentarsi allo stadio a ridosso dell'orario di apertura dei cancelli.

Si ricorda a tutti i tifosi che è severamente vietato fumare all'interno dell'impianto. Inoltre è vietato introdurre i seguenti oggetti nello stadio: stampelle, ombrelli grandi, accendini, laser, fumogeni, bandiere o vessilli non precedentemente autorizzati, caschi, passeggini, trolley, apparecchi fotografici professionali, aste per selfie e computer portatili. In caso di pioggia sarà consentito l'ingresso solo agli ombrelli portatili e senza punta. In caso di mancato rispetto del regolamento, verrà applicato il codice di condotta.

La numerazione degli ingressi dello stadio è stata modificata: non sono più presenti gli ingressi dal 7 al 10. Sul lato ovest della cancellata, in corrispondenza dei settori rossi, non sono più previsti ingressi: queste aree saranno utilizzate esclusivamente come uscite di emergenza e potranno essere aperte durante il deflusso al termine della partita.

Le persone con disabilità potranno accedere allo stadio attraverso la PC4, situata a fianco dell'ingresso 11.
